### 2026-07-31 — code-reviewer — T-043 `trd-review`, second signature (A1-A4 + B1-B7): REQUEST CHANGES, independently corroborates `developer`'s two blocking findings, adds 2 new minors

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` (commit `e142e91`) cold — read the TRD in full, then migrations `001`/`002` and `database/README.md` line by line to check the RLS/revoke idiom against the actual established pattern (not the TRD's description of it), then `hood-dataset/TRD.md` and `places-dataset/TRD.md` directly for the cross-task ray-cast question — **before** reading `developer`'s and `data-engineer`'s entries below, specifically so my read would be independent corroboration rather than a re-statement of theirs. Compared after the fact.
- **RLS/revoke security pass (the mandated primary pass on this migration) — the core design holds up under adversarial reasoning, with one real gap.** Traced the full access path by hand: RLS enabled + zero policies on all four base tables is default-deny for every role except owner/`bypassrls`, independent of any grant; `revoke all ... from anon, authenticated` is a second, independent control on top of that (removes the table-level grant itself), so a mistake in either layer alone (RLS not enabled, or the revoke omitted) is still caught by the other — correctly redundant, not just decorative. `events_public`'s `security_invoker = false` is the right mechanism for the view to read through the revoked/RLS-denied base tables (it executes as the view owner, who — as owner, with no `force row level security` set on any base table — bypasses RLS by default): this is the standard, legitimate Postgres pattern for a security-definer view as sole read path, not a workaround. Column projection correctly drops `dedup_key`, `primary_source`, `ingested_at`, `end_at_estimated`. **The one real gap, independently reached, matches `developer`'s finding 1 exactly:** `hood_for_point()` and `reattribute_events()` (§4.1) have no `revoke execute` and no stated security mode (definer vs. invoker), unlike every other function in this document (§4.3's three ranking functions all get `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated`, citing migration `002`'s HIGH finding by name). `reattribute_events()` is write-capable — it can re-run attribution across every row in `events` — so this is the identical exploit shape as `002`'s HIGH finding if the function ever ships (or is later changed to) `security definer`: an anon-key holder calling `POST /rest/v1/rpc/reattribute_events` repeatedly gets unbounded, unauthenticated write-amplification with no auth check possible, because nothing in this text rules that mode out. Confirmed by direct grep of §4.1 and §9 A3 — no revoke anywhere in either. **Concur with `developer`: blocking, needs the same revoke + an explicit security-mode statement added to A3 before build.**
- **Cross-TRD `hood_for_point()` consistency (the third instructed check) — confirmed inconsistent, not resolvable inside this TRD alone.** Read `hood-dataset/TRD.md` and `places-dataset/TRD.md`'s own text directly rather than trusting either summary: both state, in near-identical language, that the containment/ray-cast check is enforced by `database/scripts/validate_dataset.py` — offline stdlib Python, run at authoring time over hand-authored files, **explicitly not Postgres** ("There is no PostGIS on this project... a DB-level containment constraint would mean adding a spatial extension for one authoring-time check"). T-043 §4.1/§8 F7 claims `hood_for_point()` is "the same primitive `places-dataset` req 1 needs... whichever of T-042 and T-043 builds first owns the function; the other consumes it" — that framing cannot be literally true, since T-042 never calls a Postgres function for this at all. Net: two independent, necessary implementations of the identical even-odd ray-cast (Python at authoring time, SQL at ingest time) with no shared test vectors and no stated on-boundary convention for `hood_for_point()` (T-040's Python side pins on-boundary to `false` explicitly; T-043 lists on-boundary as a unit-test case in A3 without stating the expected result). Failure scenario: a place and a live event at the same coordinate on a shared Hood edge could silently resolve to different Hoods, with nothing in either test suite positioned to catch it. **This is not a new finding — `developer`'s entry below, `data-engineer`'s entry below, and my own earlier T-042 `trd-review` pass (commit `78f24a9`, further below) already reached the same conclusion independently — but three-for-three convergence from three separate reviewers is worth stating plainly as settled, not still-open-to-debate.** Routing stays `chief-of-staff`/`architect`'s: either correct §4.1/F7's wording to describe a documented cross-language conformance check (one checked-in fixture of point→expected-hood_id cases, including on-boundary, run against both implementations) or genuinely unify the two — not mine to pick.
- **`events.hood_id ... on delete set null` (§3.2) vs. `hood-dataset/TRD.md`'s explicit "should follow the same `restrict` rule" (§8 risk table) — confirmed a real, undocumented divergence, matching `developer`'s finding 2.** Formed my own view on the merits before reading `developer`'s take: I read `set null` as very likely the *more* correct choice for `events` specifically, not just an oversight — events are machine-ingested, numerous, and already designed to tolerate `hood_id = null` as an ordinary state (§3.2's own F1 discussion, `reattribute_events()` is the designed repair path), whereas `places` are curated, few, and expensive, which is exactly why `restrict` is right there. If `events.hood_id` also used `restrict`, T-040's own Hood-prune step (`delete from hoods where city = 'tel-aviv' and id not in (...)`) would fail outright the moment any live event referenced a dropped Hood id — plausible on nearly every geometry revision, since events churn constantly. That reasoning is nowhere in T-043's text, though, and T-040's audit line reads as a settled expectation this TRD silently contradicts. Concur with `developer`: needs one sentence in §3.2 stating the rationale, and T-040's audit line corrected to match rather than left claiming an FK behavior this TRD doesn't implement.
- **Two new, additive minors, not yet flagged by `developer` or `data-engineer`'s passes:** (1) **Migration-safety/idempotency.** Every `create table` shown in §3.1-3.4 (`event_sources`, `events`, `event_source_links`, `event_ingest_runs`) and their indexes are plain `create table`/`create index`, no `if not exists` guard — migrations `001`/`002` and `places-dataset/TRD.md`'s shown DDL all use `if not exists`/`create or replace`/`on conflict`, and `database/README.md`'s own convention plus this review's mandated migration-safety pass both call for exactly this. A paste-twice re-run of this migration as currently shown would error on the second run. Likely a TRD-illustrative-SQL omission, not a deliberate choice, but worth naming before it reaches the real file. (2) **`with (security_invoker = false)` needs Postgres 15+.** The view's security-definer behavior is correct by default on any Postgres version (pre-15 views always ran this way), but the explicit reloption syntax itself only exists from PG15 onward — `developer`/`ios-developer` should confirm the Supabase project's Postgres version before A2, or drop the explicit clause (equivalent to omitting it pre-15).
- **Confirmed sound, no objection, matching `data-engineer`'s read:** `rank` absolute/time-invariant (§4.3, no per-hour column anywhere in §3, `discovery-engine-spec.md` R1 correctly generalized from time-bucket to event-window); `event_sources.enabled`/`terms_verified_at` CHECK is a real structural constraint, not a doc promise; `:20` vs. migration `002`'s `:05` — checked `002` directly, no collision, and no other scheduled job exists in `database/README.md`'s status table to conflict with either.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES**, unchanged from and independently corroborating `developer`'s call below. Blocking: finding 1 (execute revoke + security-mode statement on `hood_for_point()`/`reattribute_events()`) and finding 2 (the `on delete` rationale-or-fix) — both additive, both cheap, neither a redesign. The cross-TRD F7 reconciliation is a joint precondition for build (A3 and T-042's B2 alike), already flagged three times over and not something a fourth flag resolves — it needs an owner, not another review pass. The two new minors (idempotency, PG-version dependency) are should-fix, non-blocking.
- **Left behind:** nothing beyond what `developer`'s and `data-engineer`'s entries already left — this entry exists to provide the second signature the dispatch requires (`developer` + `code-reviewer` on A1-A4, `data-engineer` + `code-reviewer` on B1-B7), reached independently rather than assumed from their sign-off.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear (both correctly out of scope for this review; `BOARD.md` is also mid-edit by another session — left untouched, not staged). Did not edit `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md`, `hood-dataset/TRD.md`, or `places-dataset/TRD.md` — all three are other agents' in-flight or already-reviewed documents. Did not stage the other modified/untracked files sitting in this working tree (`agents-mirror/*.md`, two untracked `design/*.md`) — none are mine.
- **Git:** committed to `passenger-brain` (branch `main`), explicit path only — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`. **Committed, not pushed — no remote configured**, verified directly (`git remote -v` returns empty on this checkout; L-015/`PAS-21`, still open, still Aviran's).

### 2026-07-31 — data-engineer — T-040 `trd-review`, algo/data track (B1-B6): ACCEPT, corrected cost estimate confirmed, hood_for_point() resolution independently re-derived and endorsed

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/hood-dataset/TRD.md` (commit not yet cut — current `trd`-state text) against B1–B6, specifically dispatched to weigh in on four things: the generator/pipeline-inversion design (§2.1), the upsert+prune fix (§3.3), the non-overlap validator (§2.3, §5.2 V6/V7), the final `hoods` schema contract (§3.1, §4.1), the corrected ~150-line estimate for `C-HOOD-1` clause 4, and the live hood_for_point() cross-TRD conflict. Read `places-dataset/TRD.md` §4.1/§6 and `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.1/§8 F7 directly rather than taking T-040's quotes of them on faith, and read every prior worklog entry touching this file's design before writing anything, including the two data-engineer passes already done on T-042 (line 129, REVISE→corrections) and T-043 (line 43, ACCEPT) — this is the one review of the three sibling data TRDs nobody had done yet.
- **Pipeline inversion (§2.1) — sound and the right call, checked against the actual code, not the PRD's description of it.** Confirmed independently: `export_hoods_geojson.py:92` does raise on an open ring, the bundle's rings are open and `001`'s are closed, and the script needs `DATABASE_URL` with no path around it. A DB→bundle pipeline where the DB is unreachable by every build agent is not a pipeline, it's a design that has never once executed. Inverting to a checked-in `hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` as the sole authored file, with one generator emitting both the migration and the bundle, removes the credential dependency entirely and makes the artifact byte-reproducible (`--check`, `git diff --exit-code`) by anyone. No objection to the architecture. One implementation note for whoever writes `build_hoods.py` (my own future build step, not a TRD defect): "byte-identical" across runs needs a pinned float-serialization rule (fixed decimal places or a canonical `repr`, not Python's default `float.__repr__`, which is platform/version-stable in CPython but still worth pinning explicitly in the generator rather than relying on that stability implicitly) — small, but worth stating in B2's own docstring so a future contributor doesn't "fix" nondeterminism into the generator by accident.
- **Upsert + scoped prune (§3.3) — correct diagnosis, correct fix.** Verified the claimed SQL/comment mismatch in migration `001` independently (the header comment says the export "supersedes... upsert-shaped," the actual A3 SQL is `on conflict (id) do nothing`) — the TRD's read is accurate, not overstated. `on conflict (id) do update set ...` plus `delete ... where city = 'tel-aviv' and id <> all(...)` is the right shape: the update clause covers every mutable column including `updated_at`, the delete is correctly scoped to prevent a future second city's prune from touching Tel Aviv's rows, and the cascade audit (§9, `hood_density` cascades correctly via `001`, `places` must not via `restrict`) is complete against every FK that exists or is planned. **One forward-looking, non-blocking observation not in the TRD's own risk table:** `hoods.id` is a bare global `text` primary key with no city-scoping in the key itself, only in the prune's `where` clause. A second city sourced later with an id that collides with a Tel Aviv slug (two cities each authoring a Hood called `downtown`) would hit the primary-key constraint at insert time — a loud, safe failure, not a silent one, so this isn't a correctness bug — but it means slug uniqueness is a citywide convention nobody has written down yet. Cheap fix now (city-prefix slugs at authoring time, e.g. `tel-aviv-florentin`), expensive to retrofit once `places.hood_id` and device-local Been-place state reference today's bare slugs. Flagging for whoever writes the second city's TRD, not asking T-040 to change its already-stable `id` examples.
- **Non-overlap validator (§2.3, §5.2 V6/V7) — the design is correct, and it is exactly what the invariant needs.** Independently worked through why a naive segment-intersection test cannot be used (it flags every shared edge, and adjacent Hoods share edges by construction): strict-interior containment (predicate 1, on-boundary → `False`) plus *proper* transversal crossing (predicate 2, collinear/endpoint contact → `False`) composed as "any vertex of A strictly inside B, or vice versa, or any edge pair properly crossing" is both sound and complete for the cases enumerated — two Hoods sharing a full edge pass (no vertex is strictly inside the other, no crossing is transversal), and full containment is caught by the vertex-inside test even with zero crossings. **One concrete fixture gap worth adding, not a design defect:** the listed fixture set (§11 B1: disjoint, full-shared-edge, single-shared-vertex, sliver-overlap, full-containment, self-intersecting, nested, swapped-lat/lng, out-of-bbox, 200m-hole) has no case where two polygons share a single vertex *and* one polygon's edge continues from that shared vertex into the other's interior (a T-junction/tangent-then-overlap shape) — worked through it by hand and the composition should still catch it via the vertex-strictly-inside check on the far endpoint of that edge, but it's exactly the kind of degenerate-vertex case where a sign-computation edge case in `segments_properly_cross` could silently misclassify, and it costs one more fixture entry to be sure rather than assumed. Recommend adding it to B1's must-pass/must-fail list before real data lands, per §9's own risk row ("a validator first exercised on the real dataset is a validator nobody has watched fail").
- **Final `hoods` schema contract (§3.1, §4.1) — agree with all three nullability calls, independently re-derived from the consuming PRDs rather than taken on the TRD's word.** Checked `tourist-trap-flag.md` reqs 4/7 directly: `is_tourist_trap` needs three real storage states (`null` unrated / `false` not flagged / `true` flagged) because VoiceOver announces `null` and `false` differently — a `not null default false` would collapse "unrated" into "confirmed not a tourist trap" at the schema level, which is wrong data, not just imprecise data. Checked `passport.md` req 4: no "unknown" state exists for progression-designation, so `designated_for_progression boolean not null default false` is correct in the opposite direction — every existing row is valid with no backfill. `blurb` nullable with `""` rejected at the validator (V8) rather than relying on the client to normalize is the right belt-and-suspenders call given `hood-place-detail` req 2 already forbids placeholder copy client-side. No objection; this is the same asymmetric-nullability reasoning I'd have reached independently from the same three PRDs.
- **The corrected ~150-line estimate for `C-HOOD-1` clause 4 (§2.3) — confirmed, and it matches my own independent line count.** Worked it through myself before reading the other reviews: `point_strictly_inside` (ray-cast, ~25-30 lines) is genuinely shared with containment and is the part clause 4's "same ray-cast" claim is right about; `segments_properly_cross` (orientation-sign transversal test, ~25-30 lines) and `polygons_overlap`'s composition (~15-20 lines) are non-overlap-only; V6's pairwise bbox-prefiltered orchestration (~20-25 lines) and V7's 50m sampling-grid coverage-gap clustering (~40-50 lines, the single biggest chunk — grid generation, per-cell containment lookup, contiguous-cluster flood-fill, area sum) are also non-overlap-only. That totals to roughly 150 lines beyond the shared ray-cast, matching T-040 §2.3's own number and the combined ~180-line total the T-042 reviewer independently landed on (line 136 of this file, "~180 lines across two owners"). The two independent estimates agreeing is real corroboration, not just one person's arithmetic. Clause 4's *conclusion* (one shared file, one owner for all polygon math and ring conventions) holds regardless of which of the two rationales is quoted — the estimate correction is a documentation fix, not a design one.
- **hood_for_point() — independently worked through before reading how the other five reviews landed, then checked my reasoning against theirs.** T-040/T-042's `validate_dataset.py` is offline stdlib Python over checked-in JSON, with no DB access, specifically so the authoring pipeline needs no credential — that is this TRD's entire reason for existing (§1, §2.1). T-043's `hood_for_point()` is a live `plpgsql stable` function queried inside Postgres at ingest time against the *live* `hoods` table, invoked from a cron-triggered Edge Function. Neither bridge direction is viable: a Python subprocess invoked per ingested event from inside a `plpgsql` function (or from the Deno orchestrator) is not proposed anywhere and does not belong in a scheduled hot path; the offline validator calling into live Postgres re-imports the exact `DATABASE_URL` dependency this TRD was written to eliminate. **There is a second, independent reason beyond the runtime/language split, worth naming since nobody had stated it yet:** `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §2's own architecture deliberately keeps *all* business logic — attribution, dedup, expiry, ranking — inside Postgres so `data-engineer` can retune it in SQL without a redeploy, the same property migration `002` bought for density thresholds. Moving attribution out of SQL into the Edge Function to make it "shareable" would violate that TRD's own stated design goal, independent of the cross-language problem. Both reasons point the same way: **these are two necessary, separate implementations, and I agree with the resolution already reached by `developer` (twice), `code-reviewer` (twice), and my `data-engineer` counterpart on T-042/T-043's reviews** — keep both, unify via one checked-in fixture of point→expected-hood_id cases, not shared code. **What I'd add, concretely, since I'm the one who'll write the Python side:** the fixture must pin not just the boundary-inclusion semantic (on-edge → not contained, already stated correctly on the Python side as `point_strictly_inside`'s `False`) but the exact ray-cast tie-break rule for a ray passing through a vertex — a well-known even-odd-algorithm edge case that a `(yi > y) != (yj > y)` half-open edge test handles correctly and a naive implementation can get wrong in either language independently. Propose the fixture file live at `database/data/hood_containment_fixture.json` (point, expected-hood-id-or-null, one-line description per case), read directly by the Python test suite; since this workspace has no CI, the SQL side cannot auto-verify against the same file, so `developer`'s A3 unit tests should transcribe the identical cases with an explicit comment pointing back to the fixture's path — a manual-sync discipline named honestly rather than glossed as automatic, matching this workspace's actual capabilities (no git remote, no CI, per L-015).
- **Verdict: ACCEPT the algo/data track (B1–B6) as specified.** No redesign needed. One document fix already tracked elsewhere (the hood_for_point() wording correction, now confirmed a sixth time, across three TRDs) and two new, narrow, non-blocking items from this pass: add the T-junction/shared-vertex-then-overlap case to B1's fixture list; name the fixture file's path and manual-sync-with-SQL discipline explicitly in whichever document ends up holding the final hood_for_point() reconciliation, so "shared fixture" doesn't stay an intention with no filename.
- **Left behind, for `chief-of-staff`/`architect`:** the hood_for_point() reconciliation itself still needs a board row and an owner (not new — five prior entries already said this; this is the sixth confirmation, now from T-040's own side) — recommend closing that loop before T-043's A3 or either T-040/T-042's B1/B2 actually gets written, since all six reviews agree on the resolution and nothing is left to adjudicate, only to write down in one place. The two new fixture-completeness items above are mine to build against once B1 starts, not blockers to `trd-review` clearing.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear. Did not edit `hood-dataset/TRD.md`, `places-dataset/TRD.md`, or `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` — all findings above are recommendations for whoever finalizes these still-in-review documents, not unilateral edits. Did not write any code — `validate_dataset.py`/`build_hoods.py` don't exist yet; this is a design review, not a build pass. Did not re-litigate B1–B5's already-settled points from the T-042/T-043 reviews (place_type enumeration, keyword matching, RLS, migration safety) — those tracks are covered in this file's earlier entries and are not T-040's to re-review.
- **Git:** committed to `passenger-brain` (branch `main`), explicit path only — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`. Committed, not pushed — no remote configured (`git remote -v` empty, verified not assumed; L-015, still open, still Aviran's).

### 2026-07-31 — developer — T-043 `trd-review`, backend track (A1-A4): REQUEST CHANGES — 2 new findings beyond the already-flagged hood_for_point() reconciliation

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` (commit `e142e91`) §2-§4, §9 A1-A4 against migration `001`/`002`'s idioms and `database/README.md`. Confirmed the four things this pass was dispatched to check, then read the schema line by line rather than trusting the prose summary.
- **Confirmed clean, no objection:** RLS-as-view-boundary (§4.2) — base tables get `enable row level security` with no policy plus explicit `revoke all ... from anon, authenticated`, `events_public` is the only grant, and the TRD documents the `security_invoker = false` choice clearly enough that a future reader shouldn't "fix" it back (also correctly the reason the locked-down `event_rank_floor()` call inside the view still works — the view runs as its owner, not the querying role). `rank` (§4.3, `events.rank real not null default 0`) has no companion per-hour column anywhere in §3 — matches the "absolute, time-invariant" claim structurally, not just in prose. `enabled_requires_verified_terms` (§3.1) is a real `CHECK (not enabled or terms_verified_at is not null)`, not a doc promise. Schedule (§4.4, `'20 * * * *'`) — checked migration `002` directly (`cron.schedule(..., '5 * * * *', ...)`) and `database/README.md`'s status table for any other scheduled job: none exists, so `:20` vs `:05` is the only pair in the system and they don't collide.
- **New finding 1 (blocking) — `hood_for_point()` and `reattribute_events()` (§4.1) are missing the same `revoke execute` the TRD itself mandates for the ranking functions two sections later.** §4.3 explicitly revokes EXECUTE from `public, anon, authenticated` on `event_rank_score`/`recompute_event_ranks`/`event_rank_floor`, citing migration `002`'s HIGH finding verbatim ("Postgres grants EXECUTE to PUBLIC on every new function and Supabase auto-exposes each as a PostgREST RPC... a callable `recompute_event_ranks()` is an unauthenticated write-amplification endpoint"). §4.1 defines `hood_for_point()` and `reattribute_events()` with no revoke at all. `reattribute_events()` is write-capable — it mutates `events.hood_id`, potentially across every row — so as specified it would default to anon/authenticated-callable via PostgREST's auto-RPC exposure, the identical vulnerability class the TRD names twice elsewhere in its own text. This needs a `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated` on both functions added to A3 before build.
- **New finding 2 (needs reconciliation) — `events.hood_id ... on delete set null` (§3.2) contradicts `hood-dataset/TRD.md`'s own stated expectation of this task.** T-040's TRD (§8 risk table, its FK-into-`hoods` audit) states outright: "`places.hood_id` is currently `on delete cascade` and must not be (D6); **`events.hood_id` (T-043) should follow the same `restrict` rule**." T-043 as written uses `set null` instead, with no acknowledgment of the divergence anywhere in its text. There may be a real reason to differ — events are machine-ingested and already tolerate `hood_id = null` as a normal, expected state (unlike `places`, where null attribution is a hard-fail), and `reattribute_events()` is the designed repair path after a geometry revision — but that reasoning isn't stated, and a sibling TRD explicitly reviewed alongside this one asserts the opposite behavior as settled. Needs either a one-line rationale added to §3.2 justifying `set null` over `restrict`, or the FK changed to match. Silent divergence between two TRDs that otherwise cross-reference each other carefully (migration numbering, C-HOOD-1, the shared-predicate question) is exactly the class of gap this review pass exists to catch.
- **Not new, confirmed from this side too — `hood_for_point()`'s "one implementation, two consumers" framing (§4.1, §8 F7) is already flagged as needing correction** by `data-engineer`'s reviews of both T-042 and this TRD, by `code-reviewer`'s T-042 pass, and by my own earlier T-042 review (all below, same date). T-040/T-042's actual shared implementation is `validate_dataset.py` — Python stdlib, offline, over authored files, explicitly not Postgres. T-043's `hood_for_point()` is a live `plpgsql` function against the live `hoods` table at ingest time. These are two necessary, independent implementations of one algorithm, not one shared function — already agreed across three prior reviews. Confirming rather than re-deriving: my own read matches the existing consensus, including the same open gap (T-043 never states its on-boundary result; the Python side's `point_strictly_inside` returns `False` on-boundary, and T-043's must match or a coordinate on a shared edge could attribute a place and an event to different Hoods). Nothing further to add here — routing to whoever reconciles it stays `chief-of-staff`/`architect`'s, per the existing entries.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES.** Findings 1 and 2 are new, concrete, and narrow — additive fixes (two revoke statements; one FK clause or one sentence of rationale), not a redesign. Combined with the already-tracked hood_for_point() wording/ownership correction (not mine to re-litigate), that's three fixes needed before A3/A4 are actually built. Everything else in the A1-A4 schema/RLS/view/scheduling track is sound as written.
- **Left behind:** findings 1 and 2 need `architect` to correct the TRD text (not mine to edit — another agent's in-flight document, same rule the other reviewers on this task have been following). Migration is still **blocked-on-aviran** for the apply step regardless of these findings — not reached yet since this is `trd-review`, not `build`.

### 2026-07-31 — code-reviewer — T-040 `trd-review` (second signature, backend/data tracks A1, B1-B6): APPROVE with minors

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/hood-dataset/TRD.md` (commit `2eac160`) against the approved PRD (`prds/hood-dataset/hood-dataset.md`), migration `001`'s established idiom, `export_hoods_geojson.py`, and the shipped `passenger-code/Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` — read all four directly, not summarized. Ran the mandatory `/vibe-security` pass over the TRD's schema/RLS/migration design (no migration file exists on disk yet — `database/migrations/` still holds only `001`/`002` — so the pass is against the TRD's SQL specification, same posture as the two prior T-042 reviews).
- **Independently confirmed the notable finding, byte-for-byte — the TRD's description is accurate, not overstated.** Read the actual artifacts rather than taking §1's claim at face value:
  - **Bundle vs. seed count:** `Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` holds exactly 4 Hoods (`florentin`, `kerem-hateimanim`, `neve-tzedek`, `lev-hair`); migration `001`'s A3 seeds exactly 5 (`florentin`, `neve-tzedek`, `lev-hair`, `old-north`, `jaffa`). Confirmed.
  - **3 shared ids, different polygons:** `florentin`/`neve-tzedek`/`lev-hair` appear on both sides. Compared coordinates directly — all three have materially different vertex sets between the bundle and `001` (e.g. bundle `florentin`: `[[34.760,32.050],[34.770,32.050],[34.770,32.058],[34.760,32.058]]` vs. `001`'s `florentin`: `[[34.763,32.053],[34.769,32.053],[34.769,32.059],[34.763,32.059],[34.763,32.053]]`). Confirmed, not approximate — genuinely different rectangles.
  - **Ring closure disagreement:** every ring in the bundle has `first != last` (open, 4 points each); every ring in `001` has `first == last` (closed, 5 points each). Confirmed by direct inspection of both files.
  - **The export script raises on the bundle's own format:** `export_hoods_geojson.py:92` is `if ring[0] != ring[-1]: raise ValueError(...)`. Confirmed at that exact line. The script currently reads from Postgres, not the bundle, so it wouldn't raise against `001`'s (closed) rows today — but the TRD's point stands: the two committed artifacts encode incompatible ring conventions, and the one tool that could have caught this at the DB↔bundle boundary enforces the convention the bundle itself violates.
  - **Never run, and cannot be, by this team:** confirmed via the script's own docstring ("Not run against live data in this session: no DATABASE_URL/Supabase credentials are available") and `main()`'s hard `sys.exit` when `DATABASE_URL` is unset. `database/README.md` independently states applying migrations and issuing credentials are both Aviran-gated. This is a real, already-shipped defect sitting in code that passed `qa`/`acceptance` under T-031 — accurately described, not embellished, and correctly flagged as unresolved since T-031 is still at `aviran-review`, not `done`.
- **TRD conformance:** checked every PRD P0 (reqs 1-7) against §3-§5. All resolved, and the three-column nullability contract matches the PRD's own wording exactly — `is_tourist_trap boolean` (nullable, no default, three states), `designated_for_progression boolean not null default false` (two states, no unknown), `blurb text` (nullable, `''` rejected by V8). No silent deviations; every place this TRD departs from the PRD's literal description (inverting the export pipeline, upsert-not-`do nothing`) is named as a numbered finding in §1, not smuggled in.
- **RLS pass (vibe-security, `database-security` reference): no Critical/High findings.** `003_hood_attributes.sql` is three `add column if not exists` statements only — no RLS change, no new grant. `hoods` already has `enable row level security` and one public `select` policy (`to anon, authenticated using (true))`, no write policy, from `001` A2; the new columns inherit it. Correct shape for public reference data with no per-user rows — matches `001`'s already-accepted pattern, not reinvented.
- **Migration safety: sound, additive, correctly identifies and fixes a real defect in already-written SQL.** `003_hood_attributes.sql` (schema) is idempotent (`add column if not exists` ×3) and safe under a paste-twice re-run. **D4's upsert+prune correction is the right fix and worth stating plainly:** migration `001` A3 seeds with `on conflict (id) do nothing` while its own header comment claims the real export "supersedes these rows by id" — under `do nothing`, the 3 shared ids would keep `001`'s placeholder rectangle geometry after the "real" data migration ran, silently, with a clean exit code. `on conflict (id) do update set … , updated_at = now()` plus a `city`-scoped `delete … where id <> all(array[...])` fixes this correctly and is scoped so it can never reach a future second city's rows.
- **Non-overlap validator design (§2.3, §5.2 V6): sound, and correctly avoids the naive-segment-intersection trap.** Three stdlib predicates — `point_strictly_inside` (on-boundary → `False`), `segments_properly_cross` (transversal only, collinear/endpoint contact → `False`), `polygons_overlap` (composition of the two) — correctly make touching-but-not-overlapping legal, which a plain segment-intersection test would not: adjacent Hoods share edges by construction, and a naive test would flag every real neighbour pair. Verified the composition is complete for the fully-contained case too (every vertex of the contained polygon is strictly inside the container, so predicate 1 alone catches it without predicate 3). No objection to stdlib-over-Shapely; matches `places-dataset/TRD.md` §6's rejection this TRD adopts, and one shared `validate_dataset.py` cannot have two dependency stories.
- **Real finding, not previously flagged anywhere in `PROGRESS.md`: a migration-numbering collision between this TRD's own two output files.** §3.3 and §7 both specify that T-040 produces **two** migration files — `003_hood_attributes.sql` (hand-written schema, A1) and a **separate**, generator-emitted `NNN_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` (the real data seed, B6) — and §3.3 gives a worked CLI example naming that second file `004_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql`. But the workspace-wide migration sequence, settled and cross-confirmed across three independent T-042 reviews already in this file (`003`=T-040, `004`=T-042/`places-dataset`, `005`=T-043/`live-events-pipeline`), assumed **one file per task**. T-042's own TRD (`places-dataset/TRD.md` §7.2, quoted directly) already claims `004_places.sql` as its one migration. If T-040 actually needs a second number for its data seed and takes "004" as §3.3's own example does, two different tasks write a file literally named `004_*.sql` into the same directory — the exact race condition §3.3's own text warns against one sentence earlier ("three TRDs are in flight against this same directory and a race there produces two files with the same number and no error"). The fix is a renumber, not a redesign: `003`=T-040 schema, `004`=T-040 data, `005`=T-042, `006`=T-043 (or whatever `chief-of-staff` assigns), and §3.3's illustrative "004" corrected so nobody copies it literally. Needs resolving before B6 (this task) or T-042's A1 actually writes a file — same class of pre-build coordination fix as the `hood_for_point()` finding below, not a schema or security defect.
- **Re-confirmed, not newly found: `hood_for_point()` Python/SQL duplication across T-040/T-042/T-043 stays open.** Already flagged independently by `developer` and `data-engineer` in their own T-042 `trd-review` entries below. This TRD's §4.4 (`C-HOOD-1` clause 4, quoted verbatim from T-042) still only covers the two Python/offline/authoring-time consumers; T-043's live SQL `hood_for_point()` is a separate implementation of the same ray-cast with no shared fixture yet. No new information this pass, still unresolved, still not this TRD's defect alone to fix.
- **Verdict: APPROVE with minors.** No Critical/High security findings — RLS is default-deny and unchanged in shape from `001`; the schema/nullability contract matches the PRD exactly; the upsert+prune and non-overlap validator designs are both sound and correctly reasoned. Two pre-build coordination items, neither blocking the schema itself: (1) the `003`/`004` migration-numbering collision found this pass, needing `chief-of-staff`/`architect` to renumber before B6 or T-042's A1 write actual files; (2) the already-flagged `hood_for_point()` three-way duplication, still unresolved. The artifact-disagreement finding in §1 is accurate as written, independently verified against all three source files.

### 2026-07-31 — code-reviewer — T-042 `trd-review` follow-up: 2 real §2.2 self-contradictions found, verdict stays APPROVE with minors

- **Did:** re-checked T-042's TRD against 3 points the coordinating session relayed from T-040's and T-033's just-delivered TRDs, after my initial verdict below was already committed (`78f24a9`). Verified each against the actual TRD text rather than taking the relay at face value.
- **Point 1 (C-HOOD-1 clause 4's cost estimate) — already caught, no new action.** My prior entry below already notes T-040's correction: the shared-validator conclusion holds, the "~30 lines, same ray-cast" framing understates non-overlap's real cost (~150 lines, different predicates). Nothing to add.
- **Point 2 (who owns the 4 new `Place` Swift fields) — real cross-TRD conflict, T-033's fix is correct, T-042's §2.2 needs a text edit.** T-042 §2.2 currently assigns "`Place` Swift model gains the four fields" to T-033 as one bundled amendment. Checked T-033's TRD directly: none of `place_type`/`keywords`/`is_tourist_trap`/`permanently_closed` has a reader inside T-033's own scope — they belong to T-037/T-038/T-035/T-036 respectively — and building all four into `Place` now is exactly what T-033's own D2 rule ("don't build for a feature that isn't specced yet," the same reasoning that keeps `created_at` out of its model) forbids. T-033's proposed split is right and cheap: the **fixture** (`places-tel-aviv.json`) must carry all four regardless, since `export_places.py` produces them non-null from source — that part is mechanical, not optional. The **Swift model** gains each field lazily, one per task, the turn it first gets a reader (`Decodable` ignores unknown JSON keys, so a wider fixture costs the earlier-landing tasks nothing). T-042's §2.2 row should be rewritten to say this rather than bundling all four onto T-033.
- **Point 3 (two claimed emitters for `places-tel-aviv.json`) — a genuine internal self-contradiction in T-042's own document, missed in my first pass.** §2.2 says the bundled fixture's four new fields are T-033's to ship, "owns the export, its step B2." §9 step B3 says `export_places.py` — this task's own script — produces "DB seed + `places-tel-aviv.json`, in one run" from the validated source. Both cannot be the emitter of the same file, and as written, either both tasks build a conflicting exporter or each assumes the other does and neither ships one. T-033's proposed resolution is correct and consistent with T-042's own §9: **B3 (`export_places.py`) owns the emitter — it already holds the validated source, which is the whole reason it exists — and T-033 keeps only a drift-check/round-trip assertion against the artifact B3 produces**, the same relationship T-031's `export_hoods_geojson.py` has to the bundle it doesn't author. §2.2's row needs the same rewrite as point 2's.
- **Net effect on verdict: unchanged, APPROVE with minors, but the minors list grows by two real, non-security, non-schema findings** — both are TRD-text corrections to §2.2's ownership table, not schema/RLS/migration changes, and both are cheap for `architect` or whoever finalizes the document to fix before T-033's A-steps or T-042's B3 actually execute against a table that currently tells two different tasks two different things about who ships one bundled JSON file. Neither blocks ratifying the schema itself (§3, RLS, C-HOOD-1, migration safety all still stand as reviewed below).
- **Also re-confirmed, not newly found:** the `hood_for_point()` Python/SQL duplication across T-040/T-042/T-043 (my own finding below, independently reached by `developer`'s review too per the coordinator's relay) stays an open cross-task item, unrelated to points 2/3 above.

### 2026-07-31 — data-engineer — T-043 `trd-review`, algo/data track (B1-B7): ACCEPT, contingent on the hood_for_point() reconciliation already flagged at T-042's review

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` (commit `e142e91`) against the approved PRD, `data-eng/live-events-feasibility.md` (my own earlier scoping, read for continuity — nothing in it is contradicted here), and `discovery-engine-spec.md`. Checked the four assigned points plus the hood_for_point() consolidation question.
- **Source-agnosticism (§2.2) — mechanically enforced as far as the schema can enforce it, not just stated.** No column, CHECK, view, or function outside `event_sources` rows and `adapters/*.ts` has anywhere to put a vendor name — `events.primary_source` is a FK to `event_sources.key`, not free text, so vendor identity can only ever enter through the one allowed row type. The "blocking finding at `trd-review`" language (§2.2, §8) is the actual enforcement mechanism, not tooling — there's no CI to lint it (no git remote, per L-015, named in this same TRD §6), so it's schema-structural plus review-gate, not automated. That's a reasonable substitute given the infra gap, not a weaker promise than "mechanical" implies as long as every future `trd-review`/code-review pass actually checks it.
- **`rank` — confirmed absolute and time-invariant, correctly reads `discovery-engine-spec.md` R1.** §4.3 has no recency/decay term by construction, and time fit is enforced as a hard filter (`events_public`'s `end_at > now()`, plus the client's window query param) rather than a rank weight — the same shape as R1's sunset-spot example, correctly generalized from "time-of-day bucket" to "event window," which is a legitimate extension of the rule, not a stretch of it.
- **F2 (no-end-time feeds) — escalation, not a silent default, and the right call.** Schema carries the fix (`default_duration_minutes`, `end_at_estimated`) but ships strict-drop and flags the policy trade to Aviran. Silently defaulting would misrepresent estimated data as source truth in the exact place req 2 cares most about; this is a product call about acceptable inaccuracy, not an architecture one, and the TRD doesn't quietly pick a side.
- **F3 (proximity vs. identical-order) — static centrality is the only coherent reading.** No per-user input exists anywhere in this pipeline (no identity, no request parameter that could carry a location), so viewer-relative proximity was never buildable without reopening the whole non-personalization stance the rest of the app holds. Correctly flagged as a finding rather than picked silently.
- **hood_for_point() consolidation — reviewed and NOT consistent across the three TRDs; already flagged once at T-042's `trd-review` (see this file's 2026-07-31 data-engineer entry on T-042 B1-B5), now confirmed from T-043's own text.** T-043 §4.1/§8 F7 describes `hood_for_point()` as "a shared SQL function... one implementation, two consumers" with `places-dataset`. But `places-dataset/TRD.md` §4.1 clause 3 puts its containment check in an **offline stdlib Python script** (`validate_dataset.py`), explicitly **not** Postgres, specifically because the authoring pipeline has no DB credentials and there's no PostGIS. T-043's own function is `plpgsql`, lives inside Postgres, and runs live at ingest time against the real `hoods` table for externally-sourced coordinates arriving continuously via an Edge Function — a different language, a different runtime, and a different data source than T-040/T-042's one-time authored-file validation. Neither bridge direction works: T-043 calling out to a Python subprocess per ingested event is not something any TRD proposes and doesn't belong in a cron-triggered hot path; T-040/T-042's offline validator calling into live Postgres reintroduces the exact Aviran-gated `DATABASE_URL` problem that made T-040 invert its whole pipeline in the first place. **These are two genuinely necessary, separate implementations of the same even-odd ray-cast algorithm, not one shared function** — the TRDs' own "one implementation, two consumers" framing is the part that needs correcting, not the underlying design. What should actually be shared: the algorithm spec (closed single-ring WGS84 `[[lng,lat],…]`, even-odd ray cast, bbox prefilter) stated identically in both places — which they already do almost verbatim — plus **one checked-in fixture file** of point→expected-hood_id cases (inside, outside, on-boundary, concave notch, bbox-outside-ring, shared-edge) asserted against both the Python validator and the SQL function, so a boundary-case fix in one language doesn't silently stay wrong in the other. One live gap worth naming: T-040/T-042 pin on-boundary explicitly to `false` (strict interior, "not by file order"); T-043's spec lists "on-boundary" as a unit-test case (§9 A3) but never states the expected result. That has to match the Python side's `false` or a point on a shared Hood edge could attribute an event to a different Hood than an otherwise-identical place — precisely the silent disagreement §4.1 itself warns about.
- **Verdict: ACCEPT the algo/data track (B1-B7) as specified**, contingent on the hood_for_point() wording/ownership correction above (T-043 §4.1/§8 F7, and the matching line in T-040 §2.2) landing before A3 or T-043's own attribution step is built. Nothing else in B1-B7 needs rework — F2/F3's escalations are correctly not resolved here, and the source-agnosticism/rank contracts are sound.
- **Left behind:** the hood_for_point() finding is now flagged identically from both sides (T-042's review and this one) — it needs a `chief-of-staff`/`architect` reconciliation pass and a board row before A3 (T-043) or B2 (T-042) get built. Not mine to action further; `code-reviewer` and `security-auditor` are reviewing the same TRD in parallel per the dispatch, not duplicating their scope.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear. Did not edit `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` or `places-dataset/TRD.md` — the wording correction is `trd-review`'s to action, not a unilateral edit to another agent's in-flight document. Did not write any code — no adapter, no SQL function exists yet; this is a design review.
- **Git:** committed to `passenger-brain` (branch `main`), explicit path — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`. Committed, not pushed — no remote configured (`git remote -v` empty, verified; L-015, still open, still Aviran's).

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/places-dataset/TRD.md` (commit `4d126f1`) against `places-dataset.md`'s PRD requirements, migration `001`'s established idiom, and both sibling TRDs it collides with (`hood-place-detail/TRD.md`, `hood-dataset/TRD.md`) — read directly, not taken on the architect's word.
- **D1 ratified.** `places` is defined exactly once, here. Confirmed by reading T-033's TRD directly: its `§3.1` amendment (2026-07-31) already strikes its own `003_places_and_hood_blurb.sql` DDL, states "T-033 is now iOS-only in practice," and explicitly accepts `cascade → restrict` after checking every place-reading path in its own client code depends on nothing cascade-shaped. No two-TRD divergence exists in the text as written.
- **C-HOOD-1 ratified as sound.** Its four clauses (FK+`restrict`; ray-cast containment → exactly one Hood; enforced in `validate_dataset.py`, not Postgres, since there's no PostGIS and `hoods.polygon` is `jsonb`; one shared validator file for both this task's containment check and T-040's non-overlap check) are quoted verbatim in `hood-dataset/TRD.md` §4.4 and adopted without edit. T-040's TRD does flag one correction worth noting: clause 4's rationale ("same ray-cast") is exactly true for the containment half and not true of non-overlap (a different, ~150-line stdlib predicate set) — the file-sharing conclusion holds, the stated reason under-describes it. Not a defect in either document, just read both before assuming clause 4 means "identical code."
- **RLS pass (vibe-security run, `database-security` reference): no Critical/High findings.** `places`/`place_types` both `enable row level security`, one `for select to anon, authenticated using (true)` policy each, **no write policy at all** — correct default-deny, and identical in shape to `001`'s already-accepted `hoods_public_read`. The skill's "never `USING (true)`" caution is written for user-owned rows; these tables carry no `user_id` and no per-user data, so blanket public-read is the right shape, not an oversight. No secrets, no `SECURITY DEFINER`, no service-role exposure anywhere in the spec.
- **Migration safety: sound design, not yet literal SQL (expected at TRD stage).** `create table if not exists` with the full column set, followed by explicit `add column if not exists` for the 5 columns beyond T-033's original minimal set, is the right shape to survive either build order — and moot in practice today since T-033's own migration block is already struck. Both FKs `on delete restrict` (not `cascade`) is correctly justified: a Hood-geometry revision that would otherwise silently delete curated places now fails loudly, per `hood-dataset` req 7. One build-time flag for `developer`: §7.2's "nullable → backfill → `set not null`" plus the "`do $$ … $$` guard on `pg_constraint`" branch is described in prose only — that branch (table pre-exists, columns added after the fact) is materially more complex than the fresh-create path and deserves its own test before the migration is called done.
- **Minor — migration filename should be pinned now, not left contingent.** §2's table and §2.1 both still say `NNN_places.sql` / "expected `004`... if T-040 lands second, `003` is fine." Migration numbering across the three sibling TRDs is now settled (`003` = T-040, `004` = this task, `005` = T-043) — the TRD should say `004_places.sql` outright before `developer` writes the file, not leave it as a hedge that was true only while T-040 and T-042 were racing.
- **Minor — one framing overstates the PRD.** §8 and §2.2 both describe closed-state refresh as scope the "PRD fences out." Checked `places-dataset.md` directly: req 5's third bullet ("a refresh path exists and runs on a stated cadence") is a live, unmet P0 acceptance bullet — it sits in Open questions & risks, not in the Description's `Not in scope` list. The TRD's actual handling (ship `closed_checked_at` to make staleness visible, escalate ownership rather than build a pipeline nothing specs) is the right call given no owner exists anywhere in the pipeline — but it should be described as "escalated, unowned, and unmet" rather than "fenced out by the PRD," and `product` should move this bullet to Not-in-scope or open a follow-on PRD so req 5 doesn't sit permanently unmet against its own document. Non-blocking; T-044/`PAS-22` already tracks the ownership gap.

T-043's TRD needs a **live, runtime, SQL** `hood_for_point()` inside Postgres for event-ingest attribution — a different language, a different execution context, but the same ray-cast math over the same `hoods.polygon` rings. T-043's own architect already asked, in its own worklog entry, for this to be "flagged to both this `trd-review` and T-042's" — it wasn't, in either document. Two implementations of the same geometric predicate (Python's `point_strictly_inside`, and whatever `hood_for_point()` becomes) drifting on boundary-case handling (on-edge inclusion, tie-breaking) is exactly the failure C-HOOD-1 clause 4 exists to prevent for the pair it does cover. **Needs a board item before T-043's A1 or this task's B2 is built** — either a written rule that both implementations must agree on the same boundary semantics with a shared cross-language fixture proving it, or a decision that one calls the other (unlikely to be practical across Python/SQL) is out and divergence is accepted with a stated reason.

No silent deviations found — every gap this review turned up was already named somewhere in the TRD's own text, just under-precise framing in two places (noted above).

### 2026-07-31 — architect — T-040 TRD written, ready for `trd-review`

- **Did:** wrote `prds/hood-dataset/TRD.md` against the approved PRD. Read the actual pipeline before specifying anything — migration `001`, `export_hoods_geojson.py`, the shipped `hoods-tel-aviv.json`, and `Hood.swift`/`HoodCatalog.swift`/`HoodHitTester.swift` — not the PRD's description of them. No design spec exists; pure-build/data, skips `design`.
- **The biggest finding inverts the pipeline the PRD assumed.** The PRD (and T-031's B3) describe "one authored source, two artifacts, through the existing `export_hoods_geojson.py`." That script runs **DB → bundle**, which makes the database the source of truth — and it requires `DATABASE_URL`, which is Aviran-gated. It has never been run and **cannot be run by anyone on this team**. TRD inverts it: a git-tracked `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` is the only authored file, one generator emits both the DB seed migration and the iOS bundle with **no database access at all**, and `export_hoods_geojson.py` is repurposed (not deleted) as a post-apply drift check — the one job the DB→bundle direction is right for.
- **Found a swap that would have silently failed.** Migration `001` A3 seeds with `on conflict (id) do nothing` while its own header comment claims the real export "supersedes these rows by id (upsert-shaped)". The SQL and the comment disagree. Under `do nothing`, the 3+ ids the real dataset shares with the placeholder set would keep their **placeholder rectangle geometry** after the "real" seed applied — a migration that runs clean, reports success, and leaves overlapping rectangles for `HoodHitTester` to resolve by file order. TRD specifies upsert + a `city`-scoped prune instead.
- **The two shipped artifacts already disagree, and the rings are in two different formats.** Bundle has 4 Hoods, `001` seeds 5, only 3 ids common, shared ids have different polygons. Bundle rings are **open** (`first != last`), `001`'s are **closed**, and `export_hoods_geojson.py:92` *raises* on an open ring — never caught because the script has never run. §4.2 pins one canonical wire form (closed) with `HoodCatalog` dropping the duplicate at decode, and states that both forms must decode since both are in the repo right now.
- **Non-overlap validation (PRD req 2, the load-bearing ask) is specced as three stdlib predicates, not a suggestion.** In the generator, before either artifact is opened for writing, non-zero exit, every violation printed not just the first. The key design point: adjacent Hoods **share boundaries by construction**, so a naive segment-intersection test flags every shared edge and no real dataset could ever pass — the check is strict-interior containment + *proper* (transversal) crossing, which makes touching legal and overlapping fatal. Two more tripwires: a Swift port in `HoodCatalogTests` against the shipped bundle (catches a hand-edited artifact), and the repurposed export script (catches an out-of-band DB write).
- **Reversed my own dependency call mid-write, on T-042's argument.** I had specced Shapely for exact overlap-area. `places-dataset/TRD.md` §6 rejects it by name (C/GEOS dependency on a stdlib-only project) and §4.1 clause 4 puts both invariants in **one shared `validate_dataset.py`** — one file cannot have two dependency stories. Adopted stdlib. Honest costs written down rather than glossed: no ε tolerance knob (fine — §9 argues the fix for source imprecision is vertex snapping anyway), and V7's coverage-gap check drops from exact union geometry to a 50 m sampling grid. Treated asymmetrically on purpose: overlap is approximated by nothing because `HoodHitTester` rests on it; coverage is a legibility judgement where 50 m resolves gaps an order of magnitude below the threshold that matters.
- **One correction to `C-HOOD-1` clause 4's rationale, flagged before `data-engineer` estimates.** Clause 4 says the two invariants "are the same ray-cast over the same rings." True of T-042's *containment* check, **not** of this task's *non-overlap* check — overlap between two polygons is not a ray-cast. The conclusion (one shared file) holds; the stated reason under-describes the work by roughly 30 lines vs. 150. Quoted all four clauses verbatim per T-042's own instruction, and put the correction in §2.3 rather than editing the quote.
- **Migration `003` collision resolved on both halves.** T-033's `003_places_and_hood_blurb.sql` claimed the `places` table *and* `hoods.blurb`. T-042 §2.1 took `places`; this TRD takes `blurb` (PRD req 4, product's sweep assigned it here, T-040 is first in the chain). Net: **T-033's migration `003` has nothing left in it and should be removed rather than renumbered.** This task's `003_hood_attributes.sql` is three `add column if not exists` — `blurb text` null, `is_tourist_trap boolean` null (three states, because `tourist-trap-flag` req 7 needs them), `designated_for_progression boolean not null default false` (two states, because `passport` req 4 has no unknown).
- **`places.hood_id` must be `on delete restrict`, not `cascade`.** T-033's §3.1 declares `cascade`; with this TRD's prune, a geometry revision that drops a Hood would **silently delete the curated places inside it**. Same clause T-042 states — deliberately identical wording. T-043's `events.hood_id` follows the same rule. Every current and planned FK into `hoods` audited in §9.
- **Promoted one PRD P1 with a real reason, flagged as a scope call.** `HoodCatalog.centroid(of:)` averages vertices — correct for the 4 shipped rectangles, wrong for a real municipal polygon, where a densely-sampled shoreline drags the label off-centre or outside the shape. Shoelace area-weighted centroid computed at build time, ~15 stdlib lines each side, no runtime cost. The defect only appears once real geometry lands, i.e. when nobody is looking for it.
- **Resolved the PRD's three open technical questions:** multi-ring/holes → **no, and the validator hard-rejects nesting**, with an authoring rule (simplify, or split into two slugs) and a *named escalation trigger* so "just flatten it" can't become an undocumented decision; validator location → in the generator, not CI (there is no CI here, and a post-write check can only tell you something already-committed is wrong); Been-place re-attribution → **genuinely not answered**, it's device-local state no backend step can migrate, `passport`'s own question, named in §10 rather than designed around.
- **Made PRD req 1's "no hole large enough to read as a rendering bug" falsifiable** with arithmetic from the shipped constant (`MapScreen.telAvivCityWide`, `latitudeDelta: 0.14` ≈ 15.6 km ≈ 22 m/pt): error above 40,000 m², warn above 5,000 m². Exterior coverage deliberately left as a `qa` visual check — it needs a municipal boundary the dataset doesn't have.
- **Promoted PRD P1 `provenance` to required in the source, but kept it out of the DB and bundle.** If geometry is derived from OSM, ODbL attribution attaches to the derived database, and recording provenance after the fact means re-deriving it. One object per row during authoring. No column until someone establishes attribution must be *displayed* — that's `PAS-6` item 15, Aviran's.
- **Build breakdown:** A1 **[Backend]**, B1–B6 **[Algo/Data]** (B6 also **[Backend]**), C1–C5 **[iOS]** — backend/data-heavy as expected, with a genuinely small iOS side (decode 3 fields + stored centroid, swap the bundle, one test file, one cold-open measurement).
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md`, Linear, `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`, T-033's TRD, or T-042's TRD — the T-033 amendments are named for `chief-of-staff` to route, not applied by me (that document is under another pair's review). Did not run `/grilling` — autonomous dispatch, no human in the loop to answer, per the standing rule against answering my own questions. Did not stage other sessions' in-flight files.
- **`trd-review` sign-off needed from:** `data-engineer` + `code-reviewer` (B1–B6, the bulk), `developer` + `code-reviewer` (A1, B6's SQL, the upsert/prune correction, the `restrict` finding), `ios-developer` + `ios-code-reviewer` (C1–C5, the ring form, the centroid promotion). **B1 must be reviewed jointly with T-042's B2 — one file, two authors.**

### 2026-07-31 — developer — T-042 `trd-review` (A1-A5 backend track): D1 and C-HOOD-1 ratified, hood_for_point() 3-way duplication flagged as a required pre-build fix

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/places-dataset/TRD.md` (commit `4d126f1`) against the approved PRD, `hood-dataset/TRD.md`, and `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` (both landed since this TRD was written, so I read them live rather than trusting the collision as of authoring time). Also read migration `001`/`002` and `database/README.md` to check RLS/idempotency idioms against the established pattern rather than taking the TRD's word for "identical to 001."
- **D1 (migration ownership) — ratified.** `places` is defined once, here (§2.1). Cross-checked independently against `hood-dataset/TRD.md` D7, written by the same architect in a separate pass: it reaches the identical resolution from its own side — `hoods.blurb` lands in T-040's `003`, T-033's planned `003` has nothing left and should be removed rather than renumbered, T-042 owns `places`/`place_types` in the next number. Two independently-argued TRDs converging on the same split is real corroboration, not just one document's say-so. No objection.
- **Migration numbering — confirmed `004`.** T-042's TRD already anticipated this ("expected `004`, behind T-040's `003`," §2.1) and writes the migration order-independently regardless. With numbering now resolved workspace-wide (003=hood-dataset, 004=places-dataset, 005=live-events-pipeline), `004` is the number `developer` takes at build time. Confirmed, not a new decision.
- **C-HOOD-1's four clauses (§4.1) — ratified, individually falsifiable.** Referential (FK + `restrict`, checkable via `information_schema`), Geometric (exactly-one-containing-Hood, checkable via validator output), Enforcement site (Python validator not Postgres — reasonable given no PostGIS and an authoring-time-only need), Single implementation (one `validate_dataset.py` owns both T-040's non-overlap and T-042's containment checks). `hood-dataset/TRD.md` §4.4 quotes all four verbatim and corrects clause 4's stated *rationale* (non-overlap isn't literally "the same ray-cast" as containment — it's 3 predicates, ~150 lines, not the ~30-line point-in-polygon) without changing the conclusion. That correction is sound and doesn't weaken the clause.
- **hood_for_point() — flagged as a required pre-build fix, not a "looks fine."** `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` independently builds a Postgres SQL function `hood_for_point()` (§4.1, its F7) for runtime event→Hood attribution, and its own build-breakdown table describes it as **"a shared SQL `hood_for_point()` — the same primitive `places-dataset` req 1 needs. One implementation, two consumers."** That claim directly contradicts this TRD's own committed design: C-HOOD-1 clause 3 puts the containment check in an **offline Python validator**, explicitly not Postgres, so the authoring pipeline can run without DB credentials. T-043 was written assuming T-042's check would live in Postgres too; it doesn't, per this document's own text. Net effect if nobody reconciles this: **two real implementations of the identical even-odd ray-cast** — Python (`validate_dataset.py`, shared by T-040+T-042 per clause 4) and SQL (`hood_for_point()`, T-043) — with no shared code, no shared test vectors, and no stated mechanism keeping them in agreement on edge cases (on-boundary points, bbox prefilter behavior). A silent disagreement here is user-visible: a place and a live event at the same coordinate could resolve to different Hoods. This TRD (T-042) doesn't mention T-043 or `hood_for_point()` anywhere in its text — confirmed by direct grep, not an oversight I'm inferring. Neither does `hood-dataset/TRD.md`. Recommending: `chief-of-staff`/`architect` reconcile explicitly before T-043's A3 or T-042's B2 gets built — either T-043's "one implementation" claim is corrected to describe a documented cross-language conformance check (shared fixture file of point→expected-hood_id pairs, run against both implementations), or the two are actually unified (which would require the authoring validator to call a live DB function, a workflow change this TRD deliberately avoided). Not mine to decide solo — same weight as D1's cross-TRD conflict, and it touches three tasks' build steps.
- **Schema/RLS/migration content — no objections, matches the established pattern.** `place_type` as a lookup table with `sticker_shape not null` FK (§3.1) is the right shape for making Passport req 3's totality claim structural rather than a script someone has to remember to run. `keywords text[]` matched client-side (§4.3) is forced by three PRD requirements (offline matching, no search-only dataset, 400ms budget), not a stylistic choice — server-side FTS fails all three. `hood_id ... on delete restrict` (§3.2) is correctly the deliberate deviation from T-033's `cascade` — cascade would let a Hood-geometry revision silently delete curated places, restrict fails loudly, and `hood-dataset` req 7 asks for exactly that. RLS (§3.3) is byte-for-byte the same shape as `001`'s `hoods_public_read`/`hood_density_public_read` — enable RLS, one public `select` policy for `anon, authenticated`, no write policy at all, no reinvention. Migration idempotency (§7.2) is order-safe under either build sequence: `create table if not exists` with the **full** column set, followed by explicit `add column if not exists` for the 5 columns beyond T-033's original minimal set (`place_type`, `keywords`, `permanently_closed`, `closed_checked_at`, `is_tourist_trap`) — without that second block, a prior minimal `create` would silently no-op-skip exactly the columns this task exists to add. `place_type`/`keywords` correctly go in nullable-then-`set not null` since they're `not null` with no sensible default. Two categories only, `category` inherited verbatim from T-033 — not reopened.
- **Verdict: approve**, on the condition that the `hood_for_point()` reconciliation above lands before A3 (T-043)/B2 (T-042) are actually built. D1 and C-HOOD-1 clauses 1-4 are sound as written and don't need rework. This is a coordination gap between TRDs, not a defect in this TRD's own schema.
- **Mid-review update — T-033's amendment (commit `7e33f21`) surfaced 2 more conflicts, checked against the actual diff, not the relay summary. Both confirmed real, both resolved in favour of T-033's own recommendation:**
  1. **`Place` Swift model field ownership.** T-042 §2.2 assigns "`Place` gains the four fields" to T-033 flatly. T-033's new **D7** flags this as direct tension with its own D2 rule ("don't build for a feature that isn't specced yet" — the same rule that keeps `created_at` out of its saved-state store) — correctly, since none of the four fields (`place_type`, `keywords`, `permanently_closed`, `is_tourist_trap`) has a reader inside T-033's own scope. **Resolution, adopting T-033's recommendation:** the bundled *fixture* carries all four regardless (it must — `place_type`/`keywords` are `not null` at source, and the fixture round-trips the export), but the Swift *model* gains each field only in the task that first reads it — `is_tourist_trap`→T-035, `permanently_closed`→T-036, `place_type`→T-037, `keywords`→T-038. `Decodable` ignoring unknown JSON keys is what makes a fixture wider than the model already a supported, costless state. **T-042 §2.2's line needs correcting** to drop the single-task assignment and point to per-field ownership instead — a TRD-text fix, not a schema change; flagging for `architect`, not editing the TRD myself.
  2. **`places-tel-aviv.json` has two claimed emitters inside T-042's own document — a self-contradiction, not just a T-033 disagreement.** §2.2 says "T-033 (owns the export, its step B2)"; §5 step 3 and §9's B3 both already say `export_places.py` (T-042's own data-engineer step) writes the DB seed **and** `places-tel-aviv.json` in one run. §2.2 is the stale line — §5/§9 already agree with each other and with T-033's own recommendation (its §11 B2 note: "T-042's B3 emits it… T-033 keeps only the drift rule and the fixture's use"). **Resolution: T-042's B3/`export_places.py` is the sole emitter**, matching what this TRD's own Flow and build-breakdown sections already state; T-033's B2 becomes a drift-check/consumption step only, never a second producer. Same failure class as the `hood_for_point()` finding above — two independent emitters/implementations of one artifact is exactly the risk this review keeps finding, this time inside one document's own sections disagreeing with each other. **§2.2's line needs correcting** to match §5/§9, same as above.
  3. **C-HOOD-1 clause 4 — correcting my own stated reasoning, not just noting T-040's refinement.** Clause 4 as I reviewed it justifies the shared `validate_dataset.py` on the grounds that T-040's non-overlap check and T-042's containment check are "the same ray-cast over the same rings." **That's only true of containment.** `hood-dataset/TRD.md` §2.3/§4.4 is right to correct it: non-overlap needs proper (transversal) edge-crossing detection plus strict-interior containment — three predicates, not one ray-cast — and T-040 sizes the shared file at **~150 lines**, not the ~30 my own review cited. The clause's **conclusion** (one shared file, one owner of all polygon math over the same ring format) still holds and isn't weakened by this — but clause 4's literal wording overstates why, and that's a correction to this TRD's own text, not a footnote living only in T-040's document. Flagging for `architect` to reword clause 4 to the accurate justification ("one owner for all polygon math over the same ring conventions," not "the same algorithm") the next time this TRD is touched.
- **Left behind:** the hood_for_point() flag needs a board row/owner (not mine — `chief-of-staff`'s to sequence, same as D1 was). Same for the two §2.2 text corrections and the clause-4 reword — three small TRD-text fixes, all identified, none applied by me (not my document to edit; `architect`'s). `data-engineer`+`code-reviewer` (B1-B5) and `code-reviewer` (both tracks) are reviewing in parallel per the dispatch; did not duplicate their scope.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear. Did not stage other sessions' in-flight files (`agents-mirror/*.md`, two untracked `design/*.md` — none are mine). Did not apply or write any migration file — this is a review, not a build turn.
- **Git:** committed to `passenger-brain`, explicit path only (`agent-os/PROGRESS.md`). **Committed, not pushed — no remote configured** (`git remote -v` empty, verified; L-015, still open, still Aviran's).
